### 5.2b Performance sweep by reading, 21 November 2026

Asked for by the maintainer for the sync and `-reindex-chainstate`, with memory speed as the bound and nothing already dismissed proposed again. Three readers, their leading claims checked in the code at 8b647073b9; nothing built, nothing measured, every item waits for the maintainer's word. The shares named are those of 7.9 and 7.3 and are ceilings for what an item addresses, not expected gains.

1. A store cell is 104 bytes and cells lie back to back in a region, so a cell starts at 0, 8, ... 56 bytes into a cache line and four of eight cells span three lines. `PrefetchNode()` asks for the cell's first line and the one 64 bytes on; for those four the list links (bytes 96 to 103) are on a third line nobody asked for, which `PrefetchLinks()` and `Detach()` then wait for, and for three of them the height and flags too. On the create path the key (bytes 4 to 39) lies across two lines for the same four, while one is asked for. The comment at `coinsstore.h:875` ("the link and the key are in a cell's first cache line") has been wrong since the 32-bit links made the cell 104 bytes (commit 27). Remedies: ask for the line of the cell's last byte as well (and of byte 39 on the key path), or move the links beside the chain link. Both runs.
2. The change record of a block is freed by `ConfirmBlock()`, which `PublishConnectTip()` calls on the connector under cs_main (`validation.cpp:4336`). Unless the block is marked or has owed spends the record is not read at all, but the vector's destruction runs a `Coin` destructor over every 104-byte item the workers wrote, on lines that are in the workers' caches: this is very likely the 4.8% of load-latency weight 7.9 gives `ConfirmBlock()`, and it is serial. Remedies: free the record on a worker (the block's bin), and record a create as its outpoint alone where it overwrote nothing. Both runs. The record's readers (`UnwindBlock()`, `OwedValue()`, `CollectRecords()`) fix its shape, so the second half is the larger change.
3. Witness data costs an allocation for the stack and one per item (three for a P2WPKH input, six of the nine of a two-input two-output transaction), each freed a window later. It is the largest known caller in the allocator's 21 to 26% of CPU. One buffer per transaction with the items as views into it removes them and keeps the transaction's ownership, so it is not the per-block arena that was ruled out; it changes `CScriptWitness::stack`, used in 28 files and by the interpreter. Both runs. A design decision.
4. `ConnectBlock()` builds a `PrecomputedTransactionData` (about 296 bytes) per transaction for every block (`validation.cpp:2921`), zeroed and destroyed element by element, about 1 MiB a full block. Its stable addresses are for the check queue, which a window block does not have (`RunConnectTask()` passes none). One object reused per transaction when no control is engaged. Part of the memset's 2 to 3% and one large allocation a block. Both runs; holds with scripts run.
5. The store's totals: a create-then-spend coin makes eight lock-prefixed additions to its thread's stripe (`Resized()`, `AddUsage()` and `SubUsage()` even for zero bytes, `Attach()`, `Detach()`), each behind a call to `MyTotals()`. And the 64 stripes are summed up to 18 times a block (`FlushStateToDisk()` reads two totals at entry for a tracepoint and again in `GetCoinsCacheSizeState()`, it is entered twice per published block and once per received block, and `UpdateTipLog()` reads them again), 13 of those under cs_main, each sum pulling the stripes out of the workers' caches. Remedies: deltas kept in locals over a shard visit and added once beside the `dirty_delta`; the totals read once per published block; the tracepoint's values computed where it fires. Both runs.
6. `ReadRawBlock()` zero-fills its buffer before the read overwrites it (`blockstorage.cpp:1426`, "intentional" for its other callers). `ReadBlock()` reading into a buffer that is not value-initialised saves a write pass over each block. `-reindex-chainstate` and blocks that left the block cache only. A buffer kept per worker is not proposed: reused memory was not cheaper in the pool rewind trial.
7. The undo data of a sync is measured on the worker, measured again, serialised into the hasher and serialised into the file on the effects thread, and its vectors (one per transaction) are freed there, on a thread that did not allocate them. Serialised once on the worker into a buffer, the structure freed there. Sync only (a reindex writes no undo).
8. Parked S5 (sizes and sigops from the bytes consumed) with the counts now on record: a transaction's size is walked twice in a reindex and six times in a sync (`CheckBlock()`, `CheckTransaction()`, `GetBlockWeight()` twice, `AllocateBlockPos()`, `WriteBlockAt()`), the legacy sigops twice. A half that needs no stored size: within `CheckBlock()` the per-transaction size test is implied by the block's. The share is `SerializeTransaction<SizeComputer>`, 2 to 3%.
9. Serial per-block work, small and never measured: cs_main taken eight or nine times per received block (two adjacent on the message handler, `net_processing.cpp:5528` and `HandOffBlock()`); three effects items per connected block of which two carry no effect; the `UpdateTip` line formatted and written under cs_main; the BIP34 ancestor walk three to four times a block; `WindowBytes()` summing every slot per staged block.

Read and not proposed, because the record already answers them: the view's second find in `SpendCoin()`, the `HaveCoinInCache()` before each `AddCoin()`, the txid part of the store's hash computed once per transaction (hash counts: three trials flat on the maintainer's machine, 7.9 and the lookups trial); the second `find` per spend in `ApplyBatch()` (a repeat of a probe whose first hit took the stall); deferring list membership for coins created and spent in the window (the young table under another name). A cheaper keyed hash for the store stays as 7.9 left it: read upstream's SipHash work first.

Built on 22 November 2026 on the maintainer's word ("do 1, 2, and 3 and do your local measurements of them with the normal configuration", by the numbering of the recommendation: items 1, 6 and 4 here), as three new commits on top, tip f27aa3b8ea. Item 1, 72a8390d52: `PrefetchNode()` also asks for the line of the cell's last byte, and on the key-only path for the line of the key's last byte; the cell comment is corrected and `LINE_BYTES` named. Item 6, 6d75dcb617: `ReadBlock()` reads into a `make_unique_for_overwrite` buffer through a private `ReadRawBlockInto()` that `ReadRawBlock()` shares and whose callers keep the zeroed vector; the shared part catches `std::ios_base::failure` where the upstream code caught `std::exception`. Item 4, f27aa3b8ea: the `PrecomputedTransactionData` vector is sized only with a check queue control, otherwise each transaction has its own local, in `ConnectBlock()` and `FinishDeferredTransactions()`. Gates: both builds without warnings, unit suite of 1053 cases, functional subset of ten. Measured once, in the one normal configuration and no grid (`cpu/perf3-ab-2026-11-22.log`; chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers on 16 cores, `-dbcache=1024`, `-test=bip30_off`, build-bench snapshots of 8b647073b9 and f27aa3b8ea, six alternating rounds, foreign load 0.7 to 1.1 cores in every run): before median 507.4 blocks/s (472.1 to 523.2), after 508.2 (496.7 to 525.0), a ratio of 1.00; process CPU median 53.5 s before and 52.3 s after, 0.98, inside the spread. The harness sees nothing from the three together; as with every change under 5% there, the maintainer's run decides. Items 2, 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9 are not built.

Item 2, second half, built on 22 November 2026 on the maintainer's word ("this slim change record, a create that overrode nothing is recorded as its outpoint alone"), as one new commit on top, tip 60e21746db. A create that went over no confirmed coin is no longer a 104-byte `Undo` item but a 36-byte outpoint in a second per-block list, `WindowBlock::plain_creates`; the record keeps the spends and the rare creates over a confirmed coin, so `OwedValue()`, the takeover test of `CreateLocked()` and every reader of spends are unchanged but for a test that is now always true. `ConfirmBlock()` swaps both out and frees them outside the slot's mutex, with no destructor to run for the outpoints. `UnwindBlock()` takes the record back first and the plain creates after it, each last first: the only changes of one outpoint a block can have in both lists are a plain create and a later spend of it, which keeps the old order (spend, then create). The first half of the item, freeing the record off the connector, was not asked for and is not built. No new unit test: the existing store tests unwind blocks at twenty places and pass unchanged. Gates: both builds without warnings, unit suite without error, functional subset of ten. Measured once in the one normal configuration (`cpu/slim-ab-2026-11-22.log`; chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers on 16 cores, `-dbcache=1024`, `-test=bip30_off`, build-bench snapshots of f27aa3b8ea and 60e21746db, six alternating rounds, foreign load 0.7 to 1.1 cores in every run): before median 550.5 blocks/s (536.2 to 553.6), after 581.0 (567.8 to 588.3), a ratio of 1.055 with the after faster in every round and no overlap of the ranges; process CPU median 49.2 s against 47.6 s (0.97); peak RSS median 383 MiB against 366 MiB. Just past the 5% below which the harness predicts nothing; a network sync has not been measured.

Item 3 closed for the series on 22 November 2026, the maintainer: "The witness allocations will be handled by a different pull request, not part of this series." Item 5 (the totals) estimated the same day at 1 to 2% of CPU at most from instruction costs (about 0.2 microseconds of 10.7 per transaction for the additions, 0.1 to 0.2 ms of about 17 ms a block for the sums), below the 2.3% spread of the maintainer's two runs of the day; not built, not recommended for the gain alone.

Reading pass over the worker side of the store, 22 November 2026 (after the slim change record, on the maintainer's "Ok, do it."; tip 60e21746db; nothing built, nothing measured, every item waits for the maintainer's word). The question was what else a worker writes per change that nobody reads on the common path, and what is destroyed on another thread.

- W1, spend items that need no destructor. A spend is still a 104-byte `Undo` holding a `Coin`; `SpendLocked()` converts the entry's `StoredCoin` to a `Coin` for it. Outside marked blocks and owed spends `ConfirmBlock()` reads nothing of the record, yet the vector's destruction on the connector runs `~Coin` over every item and so pulls a line per spend out of the worker's cache: what is left of the 4.8% of 7.9 after the plain creates went. With the coin kept as the entry holds it (script inline up to 35 bytes) an item is 92 bytes and trivially destructible; a spend with a longer script (rare) goes to a second list of full items. Removes per spend: 12 bytes twice, the conversion, and the connector's read. Touches every reader of the record (`LiveSpend`, `RecordCoin`, `CollectRecords`, `UnwindItem`, the takeover). Of the slim record's kind; smaller in bytes (12 against 68), equal in the destruction it removes.
- W2, no `Change` vector. `BlockApplyView::ApplyChanges()` moves every dirty view entry into a 104-byte `Change` (a spend's is empty but for the height and the coinbase flag), `ApplyBatch()` reads it once, and the vector is destroyed item by item at the end, about 1.4 MiB a full block allocated and freed per block. Applying from the view's entries directly (the view cleared after the apply instead of before) removes 104 bytes written and one destructor per change, both kinds, all on the worker. Larger in bytes than the slim record (104 per change against 68 per create), but same-thread and sequential, the cheaper kind. `ApplyChanges()` had 1.5% and `ApplyBatch()` outside the batch 6.8% of the load-latency weight in 7.9.
- W3, the record written once. Each item goes to a local vector and is moved to the record at the end of the shard visit (`Record()`): 104 bytes per spend and 36 per create a second time, in memory that is in the first-level cache. Writing in place means holding the slot mutex over the shard visit. Small; not recommended.
- W4, the index vectors (`applied`, `shard_of`, `hashes`, and `order`, `starts`, `next` in `VisitShards()`): 24 bytes per change and six allocations per apply, the same again per `FetchFor()`. Small; not recommended.
- W5, `FetchFor()`'s result vector: 96 bytes per input written, moved into the view and destroyed. Removing it means inserting into the view with the shard held, or a second pass. Next to the closed trials on how the checks get their coins (lookups trial); not recommended.

Destroyed off the allocating thread, complete as read: the record and the plain creates (connector; W1 ends the reading, the free itself stays, a freeing thread being ruled out); map nodes erased by another worker (the node depot handles them); the heap block of a script over 35 bytes (rare); for marked blocks the entries `ConfirmBlock()` erases on the connector (BIP30-checked heights only). Nothing new there.

W2 built on 22 November 2026 on the maintainer's word ("W2 seems like a decent change to me. Lets do that first."), as one new commit on top, tip 463233c89c. `CoinsStore::Change` holds a pointer to the outpoint and one to the coin (24 bytes, was 104); `BlockApplyView::ApplyChanges()` points them at the view's entries, applies, reports the creates as applied and only then empties the view; `ApplyBatch()` drops the unspendable creates from the vector it is given and has no `applied` vector any more. The store's unit tests and the fuzz target keep writing changes with the outpoint and coin in them through `test/util/coinsstore.h` (`OwnedChange`, a free `ApplyBatch()`): five type names, five calls and an include changed, so that production has no entry point for tests alone. Both builds without warnings, unit suite without error, functional subset 10 of 10, fuzz target compiled (not run). Harness (chain DX `-reindex-chainstate`, 16 workers, `-dbcache=1024`, `-test=bip30_off`, six alternating rounds, foreign load 0.6 to 1.1 cores, log `cpu/w2-ab-2026-11-22.log`): before 589.9 blocks/s (573.6-595.5), after 590.6 (574.5-601.0), ratio 1.00, ranges overlapping; CPU 46.7 s against 46.5 s, peak RSS alike. Flat on the harness, which predicts nothing either way (the three perf commits read 1.00 there too); a mainnet run by the maintainer decides. W1 not started.

W2 dropped, 22 November 2026, on the maintainer's word ("Yeah, drop it."), after his question whether it simplified the code: it did not (51 insertions against 49 deletions in four files and a 36-line test helper; one vector fewer in `ApplyBatch()`, against a new lifetime precondition on `Change` and the helper), and the harness was flat. The series is back at 36 commits, tip 60e21746db; the commit is kept on the branch `utxo-partial-order-w2-dropped` (463233c89c). Not to be proposed again. W1 waits for the maintainer's word as before.

The speed-ups folded into their commits, 22 November 2026, on the maintainer's word ("can you roll the speedups into the commits they actually belong to?"). By the lines each one changes: the slim change record (60e21746db) went into the store commit ("coins: CoinsStore, the tip's coins as a sharded table with write-out runs"), the transaction-data vector (f27aa3b8ea) into the window commit ("validation: connect a window of blocks beside each other"), the prefetch lines (72a8390d52) into "coins: the store links its nodes by 32-bit numbers", whose 104-byte cell is their reason; each of the three messages got a paragraph for it. The unzeroed read buffer ("blockstorage: ReadBlock() reads into a buffer that is not zeroed first") changes master's `ReadBlock()` and `ReadRawBlock()` and no code of the series, so it has no owner and stays a commit of its own, now the last. No conflict; the patches of the other 29 commits are unchanged (range-diff); the tip's tree is identical to 60e21746db's, so nothing was built or run. The folded changes were read at their new positions: the store commit has the same lines reading the record as the tip, and the other two use only what their commits have. No interim commit was compiled. 33 commits, tip 2d0235cbe8; before: branch `utxo-partial-order-before-fold-speedups`. The W2 commit on `utxo-partial-order-w2-dropped` sits on the old history and applies to the new tip as it is (same tree).

W2 back on the series and the W1 trial, 22 November 2026. After his run of 10:34.57 the maintainer asked for W2 as well: the commit of `utxo-partial-order-w2-dropped` was picked onto the folded tip unchanged, as commit 34, tip 63245884db, the tree he timed. W1 was then built as a trial, one commit on top on the branch `utxo-partial-order-w1-trial` (cbef835ea5), not part of the series. What it does: `StoredCoin` is split into `StoredCoinData` (the bytes and their readers, trivially copyable and destructible) and `StoredCoin` (the owner of the heap block of a script over 35 bytes); a record item (`Undo`) holds a `StoredCoinData` and is 92 bytes without a destructor (104 with a `Coin` before), pinned by a `static_assert`, so `ConfirmBlock()` frees the record on the connector without reading the items the workers wrote. A coin with a script on the heap keeps its owner in `WindowBlock::long_coins`, which the item points into (a moved `StoredCoin` keeps its block) and which is freed with the record; a lower block that takes a spend over makes its own owner; the unwind copies the coin back where it moved it. Unlike the earlier sketch (a second list of full items) the record stays one list in order. New test `long_script_coins_through_a_block_record` (read from the record, written by a run, unwound, taken over, confirmed). Checked: build without warnings, unit suite, the ten functional tests, the fuzz object. No harness run: it read 1.00 for W2, which showed on the maintainer's machine. The comparison is his run against 10:34.57 and 15528.51 s user (series with W2); W1 stays only if it clearly beats W2's 1.6% of user CPU. Mbox: 35 patches, the trial last. The maintainer's run with it took 10:26.94 (7.1; user CPU 0.984x of the W2 run) and he put it on the series the same day ("Yes, put it on."): commit 35, tip cbef835ea5, the branch moved forward to the trial's commit, the mbox unchanged.

The profile read after the fold of the three top commits, 22 November 2026 (no run, nothing built): the one share without a known cause is the workers' short sleeps, 6.4% of a worker's time in about 1,550 sleeps a second (7.11); the switch stacks were read with a 2% cut and name one cause, and the collection is from before the plain shard mutex. Candidate first step, not started: the same reports read without the cut. The largest identified share, the witness stacks at 14 to 15% of CPU, belongs to the separate pull request.

**Second reading sweep, 22 November 2026 (tip cbef835ea5; report only, nothing built or measured, every item waits for the maintainer's word and for the perf collection of the same day, script /work/parallel-validation/perf-collect-2026-11-22.sh).** Three readers; leading claims checked in the code. No share below is measured for the current tip.
- S1. **Closed on 2026-11-22, the maintainer: "S1 doesn't count" (reindex only). Not to be proposed again.** A -reindex-chainstate builds the undo data of every block and drops it: `UpdateCoins` reserves a vector per transaction and moves a coin per input, `SettleUndo` (validation.cpp:4084) then clears it when `write_undo` is false. `SpendCoin` accepts a null target in both views. Deferred transactions read their undo and keep it. Reindex only; in a sync it is item 7.
- S2. Item 8 sharper: the two serialised sizes of a transaction are known at parse time (`TxSegments`) and thrown away; `CTransaction` has spare padding for them. `CheckTransaction` and the block size test then become sums.
- S3. Witness items are zero-filled before they are read (`v.resize` then `is.read`, serialize.h:933); scripts are not. Sits next to the witness pull request that is off the series: the maintainer's call where it belongs.
- S4. **Withdrawn on 2026-11-22.** The maintainer: "I'm very apprehensive at turning CheckBlock into a stateful function, or changing its order of operation." Re-read at 7c165612cd: merging the loops changes which reject reason wins, carrying the sigops or the size out makes the function stateful, and the one change that does neither (the per-transaction size test implied by the block's) belongs to the closed S5. Nothing built. Item 7 (undo of a sync serialised once on the worker) was offered next and left: "Lets leave that for now too."; it stays open, not closed. `CheckBlock` walks the block's transactions five times (merkle, size, coinbase scan, CheckTransaction, sigops); folding passes changes which reject reason wins unless ordered with care. Addition to items 8 and 9.
- S5. `MAX_CONNECT_WINDOW` is 64, so 32 workers have 32 blocks behind 32 running; `MAX_WINDOW_SLOTS` is 128 and the maturity assert allows up to 99. Late sleep for lack of blocks was 1.9% (7.4), so small unless the switch data says otherwise.
- S6. The store map grows only at one coin per bucket (coinsstore.h:1027) while only the first cell of a chain is prefetched; growing at half load or a second prefetch pass. Second-cell miss rate never measured.
- S7. Detach writes the dirty-span bucket, which the links prefetch does not ask for (three lines).
- S6 and S7 read again, 22 November (no build, no measurement). S6: the map is chained, a new cell goes to the head of its chain, and all 256 shards hold the same number of coins, so the whole store moves between half a coin and one coin per bucket together. By the Poisson chain lengths a create (which walks the whole chain to find nothing) reads past the first cell in 9% of cases at half a coin per bucket, 17% at three quarters and 26% at one; a spend or lookup of a coin of random age in 21%, 30% and 37%, less for the young coins most spends take, since they sit at the head. Each such read waits for main memory with the shard held. Calculated, not counted; the time share is unknown. Growing at half a coin per bucket would make these 3 to 9% and 11 to 21% for 4 to 8 bytes more per coin (against 147), counted in `-dbcache`. Not in the trial record. S7: a span is 1024 heights and a bucket 16 bytes, so the dirty-span arrays are about 15 KiB a shard and 4 MiB in all on a run that writes nothing out: the line `Detach()` writes is in a cache, at worst another core's, never in main memory. Smaller than first written. S7 is on the series as commit 36 (274baabfc8, `PrefetchDetach()`), on the maintainer's word ("A one-line tiny change still seems fine to me"); unit suite and a short functional subset pass, no gain measured. S2 is on hold on his word of the same day. S6 by growing at half load is closed: "If S6 costs more memory per coin, that is out of the question." Never propose a map change that costs bytes per coin; the second prefetch pass, which costs none, is untried.
- S2 and S3 as a trial, 22 November, on the maintainer's word ("do them as experimental changes just to see how much they give us. I will discard them again"): branch `utxo-partial-order-sizes-trial`, two commits on the series tip 274baabfc8, e652cec1f7 (a transaction read from a buffer keeps its two sizes; `CheckTransaction()`, the block size test, the 64 byte test and the weight functions read them) and 71f085f428 (a byte vector read through `TxSegmentRecorder` is built from the buffer by `ReadPtr()`, not resized and read into). Neither changes the witness structure. Sent as patches 37 and 38 of the one mbox. A short unit and functional check passes; no timing taken here. The maintainer's run decides; the series branch stays at 274baabfc8.
- Apply-only experiment, 22 November, on the maintainer's word ("create a separate commit on top that skips actual validation as executed during a reindex chainstate. I want to see how a pure \"reindex\" operation would perform"; then "No hidden option. Just skip the checks." and "This is a dirty experiment, the tests are obviously going to fail, lets them. Just make sure it doesn't corrupt anything"). Branch `utxo-partial-order-apply-only-trial`, one commit 9f7194a605 on the series tip 7c165612cd, not part of the series. A block whose index entry is BLOCK_VALID_SCRIPTS (every block of a -reindex-chainstate) is applied without CheckBlock(), the BIP30 scan, the input amount and maturity checks, BIP68, the signature operations, the scripts and the coinbase amount; the read and parse, the deferral, the undo build and the coin write-out stay. No option. A block not connected before is checked as usual. Index safety checked once on regtest with a throwaway script (not committed): after two reindexes (window on and off) the tips and every header of every branch, a stale branch and an invalidated block included, were as before, the coin set hash was the same, and a restart without reindex passed verifychain level 4 over all blocks. Existing tests were not run and are expected to fail where a known block must be rejected on a second connect. No mainnet timing yet: the maintainer runs it.
- Result of that trial: 10:33.99 against 10:26.94, user CPU 1.004x (7.1). S2 and S3 give nothing measurable and are dropped with the trial branch, as announced; the estimate of 1 to 2% of wall for S2 was wrong. Not to be proposed again. S7, measured only together with them, shows no gain either.
- S8. Large per-block frees reach glibc's trim path (`_int_free_maybe_trim` 2.38% on 17 November); first step is a no-code run with `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.trim_threshold=1073741824:glibc.malloc.mmap_threshold=33554432`. Tunes glibc, swaps nothing; the maintainer's call whether it falls under the allocator ruling.
- S9. Small: sorted txid copy per deferring block; validation caches consulted by window blocks; `create_directories` on a read-only block file open; `own_txdata` built with scripts off; `Map::erase` computing an iterator nobody reads; an `Undo` item filled for plain creates; shard mutex on the map header's line; two `FlushStateToDisk` entries per published block.

### 5.3 Open before delivery

The plan for a safer second implementation (state machines, published copies, preconditions; drafted 15 November after round 12) is `/work/utxo-partial-order-safety-plan.md`. It was carried out from 16 November on as the machines port, and the current cut is its consolidation (head of the document).

Open, for the current cut:

- The kernel library: built on the maintainer's machine throughout, with the full functional suite (stated 2026-11-21); never built on the harness host for the current cut.
- Fuzzing: `fuzz/coinsstore_window` exists; whether it reaches the shard's own table was open on the first cut and is not checked on the current cut (no coverage report made). A sweep was made by the maintainer on 2026-11-21 (address and undefined sanitizers; the corpus of 16006 files replays clean, edge coverage flat at 2199; also `connect_block` and other targets, no finding; full-r13-verified.md section N).
- C3's oracles (section 2): the tip bench through the store exists; its warm-scripts variant and the levers of 5.2a are not started.
- `interface_usdt_utxocache.py` on a build with USDT tracing: never run against the store; set aside by the maintainer on 2026-11-21 ("Don't worry about usdt"), not to be raised again.
- The test gaps of 5.5.
- Left open by the first cut's last round, re-read on the current cut on 2026-11-21 (report only, nothing changed):
  - T2 **fixed on 2026-11-22** (cleanup pass, tip a1747de620): "16 workers" dropped from commit 26, "on eight cores" from commit 10, "at 8 cores" from commit 16, whose table of blocks per second is now ratios of the unpruned rate (0.80 to 0.87, 0.64 to 0.76, 0.73 to 0.90 at four, eight and fourteen cores; the first change alone 0.73 and 0.79), and the sentence "The block rate follows the smaller of cores and threads" removed from commit 13. Before: T2 stood as commit-message text: "16 workers" (`afdeab4293`), 8 cores and a 4/8/14-core table (`17b654ff91`), "eight cores" (`c9c6400c3b`), and "The block rate follows the smaller of cores and threads" (`a5f31069cb`), the one sentence that states a rule from one machine class.

The file is master's again from commit 13 on and commit 13's message says one existing test changes, not two. Not built at the intermediate commits. Before: the `chainstate_write_tests` comment is probably wrong for its test: `write_during_multiblock_activation` runs outside initial block download (the fixture's clock sits at the block times), so the window and its flush-before-tip order do not apply, and master's original assertion may still pass, which would make the test change unneeded. Only a run of the case with the original assertion decides; not run.
  - C2 **closed on 2026-11-22** (maintainer: "just document both of them. Don't seem worthwhile to fix"): the `Reading::BLOCK_COIN` comment in `coins_entry_machine.h` now names the kept coin and says a reader sees neither coin while the block is open (commit 11). Not to be raised as a fix again. Before: C2 stands by definition: a reader sees nothing for an outpoint whose confirmed coin an open window block overwrote, and the `Reading` comment does not name the kept coin. Reachable only by the two BIP30 duplicates.
  - H3 cannot be recovered (the raw round-12 findings are deleted). One leftover of an earlier subject was seen on the way: the block-file loader's `m_block_cache.Insert()` passes no size, so the block is measured under `cs_main`. **Fixed on 2026-11-22** in commit 13: the loader notes the two heights under the lock and inserts after it. Cleanup pass checked by one build of the tip, unit suite 397 of 397 (one skipped as on master), `feature_reindex`, `feature_loadblock`, `feature_block`, `feature_pruning`, `p2p_compactblocks`, `feature_assumeutxo`.
  - X4 **closed on 2026-11-22**, same word: the comment at `HandOffBlock()` says the bound is a count with no account of bytes, at most one message of `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` and its parsed block per task, growing with `-prevoutfetchthreads` (commit 14). Not to be raised as a fix again. Before: X4 stands: receive tasks are bounded by count (`LockTaskLimit()`), with no byte account for their messages and blocks.
- Which measurement batches run on the maintainer's machine. A pruned sync is prepared on the harness (chain DX, 6.3) and waits for the maintainer's go.

The decisions of 15 November recorded here before (fixes, closures and rulings of the first cut's rounds 8 to 11) are in the first-cut review record (4.11); the costs accepted among them are in 5.7, the test gaps in 5.5.

Redundant checks and conditions, 21 November 2026 (the maintainer: "see if you can shave off and remove redundant checks and conditions"; tip 8b647073b9, folded into the owning commits, unit suite of 1053 cases and the functional subset of ten pass). Taken: in the store, `SpendEntry()` returns nothing (its result was always true), `BeginBlock()` no longer clears a record that is empty, `CreatesPendingBelow()` loses a bound test and a `HasBlock()` its callers guarantee, `ConfirmBlock()` and `UnwindBlock()` no longer reset a `dirty_delta` that `BeginBlock()` sets, `UnwindItem()` and `CollectRecords()` lose a conjunct implied by the one beside it, `WipeShard()` no longer nulls links of nodes it frees, two `Assert`s repeated a test made one line before, and `NodeAllocator::Number()` loses a null test no caller needs; `StoredCoin`'s comment now gives the right sizes (48 bytes against a `Coin`'s 56, 36 bytes inline there). On the receive side `HandOffBlock()` asserts the peer state once and `WaitForPendingBlocks()` waits without a second mode test; `TxSegmentRecorder::Begin()` no longer resets a fresh object. In block storage `HasStatus()` and `PrepareBlockIndexBatch()` lose an implied conjunct each and a commit is no longer wrapped in an `Assert`. In validation the member `ConnectBlockResult::coinbase_check_deferred` is gone (it always equalled `!deferred_txs.empty()`; five test checks beside checks on `deferred_txs` went with it), `RunConnectTask()` loses five tests of `staged.sync` (set for every windowed block), `PublishConnectTip()` takes its phase from the staged block instead of a parameter, and `DrainWindow()` loses two tests. Left, each for a reason: the `limit != 0` of the undo backlog (the ledger's contract), `state.IsValid()` in the deferred loop, `HasBlock()` at the target-bound unwind and in `DrainWindow()`, the two phase asserts, the deliberate `Assume`s of `DisconnectTip()` and `PublishConnectTip()`, the repeated `SettleWriteOut()`, `prune_recorded`, the forced `final` of `StartWriteOutRun()`, `Assert(!store.RunInFlight())`, the teardown guard, the spentness re-tests of `UnwindItem()`, the unspendable filter of `ApplyBatch()`, the mode test repeated between caller and callee on the receive side; merging `windowed` and `sync` would be a design change, not a removal.

### 5.4 Parked

Entered once: job 2's leftovers, all resolved on 21 November 2026, see 8.4 (one final write path in place of the wipe; the test-only `PrepareWriteOut`; two heights folded into one; the index commit guard; the unread block-index states; `-connectwindow` as a knob; the instrumentation; 8.4); T3, the plain shard mutex (equal on 16 cores, 38 lines less; taken on 22 November, 5.2, and so off this list); the depot at its cap late in a run (cause not counted); a window wider than 64 (a change to the slot table); one parked trial (the front stall, 15 lines, fails an existing test); the batched fetch for a continuation and input coins by position were on the series as commits 46 to 48 on 14 November and dropped the same day without a mainnet gain (9.3); input coins by position left this list for the series on 14 November, and the skip of the second tier probe was measured and dropped (7.3).

From the comparison round of 7.7, on paper only:

- P7, the node's own hints: built on 2026-11-18 and parked on branch `utxo-partial-order-spend-hints`; the maintainer finds them fairly useful for IBD. The design is in 4.12. Not on the series; a figure for a network sync at a small cache is still to be recorded.
- P8, outputs first: a block's creations are inserted before its spends and a miss is final once every lower open block has published its creations (a watermark). It would remove deferral's wasted database read; it changes the centre and was not built.
- P9, sorted immutable runs by age with a hash-prefix directory in place of the node table (Hornet's shape): rewrites 4.2; paper only.
- P10, a salted aggregate of creations minus spends (SwiftSync's check) as a test oracle: cheap, optional, not built.

From the survey of other implementations (four readers: the other Bitcoin nodes, the Bitcoin Cash and BSV lineage, other chains with low-level techniques and their upstream state, rbitcoin; sources in 10). None of the candidates has a gain measured by its authors; the readers' claims were checked here only where they touch this code. On paper only:

- S1, a second request for the lowest missing block. Gocoin (up to three copies of the lowest blocks), rbitcoin (up to four peers, chosen by queued requests over measured rate, only once the queue behind the hole is a quarter full, far requests held while the hole is open, a silent request moved after 30 s), the BSV node and BU (a re-request without a disconnect) all do it; libbitcoin and Floresta drop the relatively slowest peer instead. It is the "second request" alternative of the stalled-front row in 5.2 and, unlike the front-stall trial (7.5), disconnects no one earlier, so `p2p_ibd_stalling.py` would keep its assertion. rbitcoin's own defect is the caveat: a getdata cannot be cancelled, so the copies a peer still owes must be counted, or the node floods itself (77 to 85% of peers' upload re-sent, by its changelog).
- S2, a request budget in bytes (rbitcoin): bytes queued plus a reserve per outstanding request from the largest of the last 32 block sizes; a requested block is never dropped for budget and a reader never parked. One rule for all heights. It touches the uncounted receive tasks of 5.1; the counted budget for parsed blocks was dropped (8.1), this one counts requests.
- S3, Schnorr batch verification with one batch per worker and a single-verify fallback. The window already runs a block's scripts inline on its worker (4.2), so a batch needs no shared queue and may span blocks. Gated on secp256k1 1134 (open); Core 29491 is a draft, and its one sync-level measurement, of a batch behind a mutex, was slower than master. Its reach is taproot signatures above the assumevalid block; that stretch was 9.7% of the connect in 7.1 and its taproot share is not known.
- S4, a huge-page hint for the store's node memory: nothing upstream, never measured anywhere; one of the few ideas that acts below assumevalid. On the series as commit 44 ("coins: ask for huge pages for the store's nodes and bucket arrays", hash in 9.3), a trial that reverts on its own: the node depot carves its lists from 32 MiB regions aligned to 2 MiB and marked `madvise(MADV_HUGEPAGE)`, a bucket array of 2 MiB or more is allocated the same way, the depot holds every list it is given (no longer at most 64) and a thread that ends hands its nodes to it, so node memory never returns to the general allocator; the accounting is unchanged. Measured on the maintainer's machine on 15 November (mainnet -reindex-chainstate to 975073, -dbcache=30000, 32 connect workers, -par=16): 13:52.88 with the commit (20099.15 s user, 1282.61 s system) against 16:12.90 without it (24598.83 s user, 1080.51 s system), 1.17x on 0.82x the user CPU; kept. Before that: its gain is not measured: the harness store is small, so the measurement is the maintainer's (the command of 6.2 with and without the commit). Checked here on the empty chain with glibc's own hint off: master 0 kB of `AnonHugePages`, the commit 6144 kB, the nodes of 40,000 coins. Two facts for reading any result: this box (aarch64, glibc 2.43, huge pages in "madvise" mode) has glibc hint all of malloc's memory by default (master: 137 MiB huge of 166 MiB resident on the empty chain, 0 of 74 MiB with `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.hugetlb=0`), so harness figures from this box were taken with huge pages already; that default is glibc 2.43's for AArch64 only (its NEWS; Ubuntu 26.04's package changelog carries no change to it), so on x86-64 malloc memory is unhinted unless the tunable is set; and `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.hugetlb=1` (glibc 2.35 or later) on the 43-commit binary is the cross-check that needs no code.
- S5, sizes and legacy sigop counts taken from the consumed bytes while deserialising (rbitcoin's precompute); the txid and wtxid are already hashed from the bytes here (7.4). Small, never measured.
- S6, a block's prevouts read in key order (the BSV node sorts them; a code comment is its only evidence). Not checked against the fetch tasks here.
- Looked at and not taken: outputs first (BU and BCHN run it as two serial loops, Flowee keeps the insertion serial because it contends and drops the order check before CTOR, Teranode's variant sits in a disabled path with no rollback; no measured gain anywhere, which leaves P8 where it is); dependency groups inside a block (BSV; the window supplies that parallelism); a per-block arena for parsed blocks (libbitcoin; `CTransactionRef` outlives the block); script template fast paths (rbitcoin's findings log has eight consensus defects in them); a store other than LevelDB (BCHN's LMDB trial: 3 h 06 against 3 h 11, not significant); libbitcoin's speculative signature capture and GPU batch (a second mode; off by default on the CPU in its own code); raised thread priority; an io_uring read path; fuse filters (they save misses, which the sync's lookups rarely are).
- rbitcoin's findings on several blocks validated as one unit, as a checklist for this design: an input bound to a creator in a higher block (its 087), the residue of a rejected speculative application (114), and the point where a missing input becomes invalid (089). Here these are the conflict and displacement rules of 4.2, I8 and deferral by prefix; the unit cases `higher_block_creating_first_is_charged`, `fresh_spend_marked_for_lower_create` and the two oracles stand for them (by their names and comments, not re-read for this).

On punishment with a receive pool, the maintainer's condition: the checks still make the peer disconnect at once on failure. The verdict travels by block hash and peer id (`BlockChecked`, `mapBlockSource`, `MaybePunishNodeForBlock`); `Misbehaving` sets a flag and the disconnect is the first thing `SendMessages` does.

### 5.5 Never measured, never exercised, no test

Statements of this kind stand where their subject is; the ones with no other home:

- Never exercised (first cut; on the current cut `feature_dbcrash.py` passed on the maintainer's machine on 2026-11-21, seed 7645990206430362434): crash recovery under load (dbcrash), the kernel library build, anything above 16 cores or 16 threads on the harness.
- No test exists for three interleavings (a reference after a window spend, the old-file sync barrier, prune under a paused write): each needs a pause hook in production code; the maintainer keeps them untested (15 November, "Keep the test gaps"). The low-severity review items (C4, C5, V5, D3 and the known design costs) stay as they are on the same day.
- Test gaps the first cut's reviews found and kept (4.11), audited against the tests at tip f80394c8f9 on 2026-11-22 and, where a test needed no hook in production code, given one in commit 33 (fb1255d0f0, since folded with commit 32 into commits 11, 13 and 14; unit suite 397 passed, the four stepping cases 48 times under load, the functional test beside `p2p_ibd_stalling.py` and `p2p_ibd_block_handoff.py`):
  - Closed before the audit, by tests the later rounds added: the window stops running continuations; the retry alone; the unwind of a drained window; a failed read; a multi-threaded unit test of the store (commit 32); `feature_async_undo.py`'s crash step; `feature_coins_lag.py` failing when its crash hook does not fire; the functional suite beyond one pool thread (`p2p_ibd_block_handoff.py` at four); a deferred and a non-deferred transaction on one coin; deferral with coinbase sigops (`deferred_checks_fail_as_in_order`).
  - Closed by commit 33: that the window defers at all and that it does not over-reject, both through the counts `~ChainstateManager` logs, which a fixture base reads after the fixture is destroyed (`WindowSummarySetup`; the line counts the fixture's two blocks connected in initial download as well); an interrupt with the window open (`interrupt_ends_a_window_step_at_a_published_block`); the conflict that gets no verdict, with `PublishConnectTip()`'s conflict and retry (`write_out_under_open_blocks_costs_a_retry_and_no_verdict`: a periodic write-out at the step's first block makes the second block's coinbase create a conflict under BIP30, one conflict, one retry, block valid); a crash after a partly written run (`coinsstore_tests/streamed_run_cut_at_any_write_is_completed_by_a_replay`, every write of a 20-write run cut in turn; it models the replay as a cache on the database applying the blocks above the old head, not `Chainstate::ReplayBlocks()` itself); the hand-off with stall handling (`p2p_ibd_stalling_handoff.py`, the master scenario at four workers, asserting the blocks were handed over); deferral with fees out of range (`deferred_fee_adds_to_the_fees_checked_at_once`).
  - Still untested, each for want of a pause hook in production code: `feature_async_undo.py`'s read step (a read of undo data while its write is still queued needs the executor held); a getdata deferred while its block is being stored, which `p2p_failed_block_not_served.py` does not reach (the lock order there is unverified by any test).
- Never measured: how uneven shards are on mainnet; the cost on mainnet of a deferred transaction's wasted database miss in core; more workers than cores; the script stretch above assumevalid in the window; one block with far more script work than its neighbours.

### 5.6 Closed by the maintainer, not to be raised again

- Rulings of 2026-11-22, after the test gaps commit (tip fb1255d0f0):
  - glibc tuning (S8 of 5.2b, and the tcache and page-cache runs of the 5.2 table): "I don't want to do more glibc optimisation. Lets leave that for the future once this series actually lands." Not to be raised before the series lands.
  - The store's totals (item 5 of 5.2b): the maintainer thinks they can go at some point and need not be kept indefinitely, but they stay for now. "Don't bring them up again."
  - Item 9 of 5.2b and S9 stay as they are: "doesn't seem material enough to spend more time on."
  - Item 7 of 5.2b (the undo data of a sync serialised once on the worker) left as it is, on the recommendation given: no effect on -reindex-chainstate, sync gain never measured, a second shape beside the solo block's structure, and a change to the undo write path. "Ok, lets leave it as you recommended." Not to be raised again unless the maintainer does.
  - The maintainer ran the full functional suite and `feature_dbcrash.py` on the tip fb1255d0f0. Not to be asked for again unless a change is material (production code that changes what is written, replayed or connected; not tests, comments, messages or a re-cut with the same tree).
  - Branches pruned on his word ("Yes, prune those side and backup branches"): 33 deleted, the series, `utxo-partial-order-baseline` (the harness baseline, 6.3) and `utxo-partial-order-v1` (the first cut) kept; the first cut, 2b58bf4cea, was deleted later the same day on his word ("Yes, delete the first cut."). Names and tips as deleted, for the record (the objects go with the next garbage collection; a reference elsewhere in this document to one of these branches is to a branch that no longer exists): `-apply-only-trial` 9f7194a605, `-before-accept-split` 460a447cef, `-before-author` 75e4cb5489, `-before-cleanup` 7c165612cd, `-before-closing-read` 46aeb1fd19, `-before-compact` 343b027776, `-before-consolidation` 3890c10625, `-before-docs` a1747de620, `-before-drop-shardapply` a6b5d9d595, `-before-drop28` 1bf147bc0e, `-before-fold-recent` 274baabfc8, `-before-fold-speedups` 60e21746db, `-before-full-r13-fixes` c600e4b1f9, `-before-guard` d1f25d340c, `-before-r13-tests` 3e81c58015, `-before-r14-fixes` 9bfd5b7825, `-before-recut` f0517a683e, `-before-redundant-checks` 87d0a6a1a6, `-before-reorder` 04ee0beffc, `-before-threaded-runs` 29ed3b4bc9, `-before-tipfixes` 890928840b, `-before-undo-cases` 3c224b4d8b, `-compact-trial` 5cc5657f49, `-lookups` 917f1777ea, `-matching-trial` e8c9e32e81, `-recv-lock-trial` 4217d2d819, `-sizes-trial` 71f085f428, `-span` 1eba1f69fb, `-spend-hints` b1d6cc0fd5, `-spend-hints-trial` ded5544e01, `-w1-trial` cbef835ea5, `-w2-dropped` 463233c89c, `-young-table-trial` 7abcf2575f.
- The coins database's LevelDB cache is not resized while running (CCoinsViewDB::ResizeCache removed with the connect window): with a snapshot both databases keep about their opening share, at most MAX_COINS_DB_CACHE (8 MiB) more than intended, until restart. Accepted (15 November, the maintainer: "It simply doesn't matter whether there is an extra chainstate with like 8MiBs more of memory. Completely inconsequential."). Never raise it again, in reviews or elsewhere.
The slow profiles of the benchcoin run without the receive pool are an outlier, and no re-run is asked for (7.5). No perf on the development box. No sync from public signet peers. Resident memory of the highest cache size plus about 2 GiB is fine (7.6). No further conditions on when the window opens (C2). LevelDB is not tuned. The low-end write-out logic stays. A short phrase with two possible referents is asked back before it is recorded as a decision (8.2).

### 5.7 Regressions, known and expected

The list for pass 3 at 82a1b90f76. The heavy-block sync and reindex rates show none (4.9, 7.7). The benches were not rerun after the fold of 4.11, so the two items the fold brought are unmeasured.

Measured, against master (figures in 4.9):

- Empty blocks at two cores: 0.78x to 0.96x of master's rate; ahead of master from four cores. The branch spends about 1.4x master's CPU on an empty block's own path and spreads it over threads that two cores cannot all run; the code that holds the extra cost is not identified (4.9).
- Resident memory: higher at harness scale at two and at sixteen cores; the window's blocks and the block cache are outside `-dbcache`.
- CPU on a sync from one peer: idle connect workers cost CPU, the same rate for 0.75x the CPU with two workers and a window of four. Cause not read.

Expected from the code, never measured:

- (Gone on 14 November: the forced coins write before a reorganisation; see the third review's fixes.)
- Outside initial block download `PublishConnectTip()` waits for the effects executor and applies its completions (the fold, 4.11), so the undo write is on the connect path again at the tip. Master writes undo on that path too: parity with master expected, a loss against the series before the fold possible.
- A PING is answered once the peer's own block tasks are done and the connector has attempted every activation signalled before the ping (the pending pong, accepted below): a delay of that peer's messages during a sync, documented and kept.
- The prune gate after the fold (4.10): the figures of the prune rule were measured with a run for each block file; the gate now makes no run below the prune target. Equal or better expected, not rerun.

Accepted, each with its lever if it is taken up (decided 15 November on the first cut; the current cut carries the same design):

- The node depot keeps its peak (the maintainer: "maybe we just need to accept this for now"): freed nodes stay in the depot for reuse, so resident memory stays at the initial block download peak, at most about `-maxmempool` above the budget once the mempool takes back its allowance. The lever: when the store shrinks below that peak, evict clean entries by region (the emptiest 32 MiB regions first, occupancy read from the depot and the threads' lists) and release a region once no live node is left, skipping regions with nodes in a thread's own list; no node moves. Compaction and a full flush-and-drop at the end of initial block download were weighed and set aside. Not measurable with -reindex-chainstate runs, which never leave initial block download.
- Freed blocks in idle per-thread bins ("accept that one for now"): a thread that builds no further block keeps what came back to its bin; after initial block download every `ActivateBestChain()` empties all bins, so only a node that connects nothing afterwards (an offline node after a reindex) holds up to a window of blocks until shutdown.
- The pending pong, documented and kept (no test-only machinery to preserve master's send-and-ping meaning). In the current cut a ping's answer waits in `Peer::m_pending_pong` until the peer's own block tasks are done and every hand-off to the connector made before it is done (`ChainstateManager::HandOffsDone()`), and no further message of that peer is taken meanwhile (`net_processing.cpp`); master's pong waits only behind that peer's own earlier blocks. Far inside the 20-minute ping timeout; the throughput lost in initial block download is not measured. Candidate fixes: an immediate pong in production with the hold behind a regtest `-test=` option the functional framework sets, or the peer's messages kept flowing with only its pong waiting.
- C5's replay cost (C5 amended, option A): a replay after a crash reads only kept blocks because pruning is held at the written head's fork with the chain, with a write-out run first where that holds a file back; the pruned node's lag cap is gone for good. Cost: a crash on a pruned node with a large cache may replay thousands of blocks at startup, as master after a long flush interval; gain: about half the coin writes on a pruned sync (4.10).
- A deep reorganisation's memory (D3): a step that disconnected blocks checks the coins cache only in `FinishConnectStep()`, so its connects can take the tier over the critical budget by about the depth times one block's changes; master checks per block, but also holds a reorganisation's new branch in `connected_blocks` outside `-dbcache` until the block that passes the old tip's work (its step returns there, `ActivateBestChainStep()`), so about the same depth (corrected 2026-11-18 against e211dd0186; this said master held the whole new branch). The lever (option B, which the maintainer liked): in a step that disconnected blocks, end the step after a connected block that leaves the cache critical, and let the next step carry on along the branch.
- The kernel's validation callbacks may run on the effects executor's thread and must not call back into the chainstate manager (V8, option B: the kernel interface is experimental, a documented break is fine); one sentence in `bitcoinkernel.h`, no code change.

The reach of all of it: the three commits of pass 3 were measured on the regtest heavy chain only, level or better against the 40 commits (7.7); no mainnet or real-network run of them exists, and nothing above 16 cores (5.5).

## 6. How to measure

### 6.1 Method

The maintainer: "I just don't believe that this is the ceiling already." And: "you are allowed to write your own instrumentation and benchmarks, as long as they don't take long and don't slow you down. Are you really sure you need all these probes?" Kept from this: a reference on the same box (master on the harness chain), counts of work per transaction and not only timers, and trying the change itself on a throwaway branch; kept when it beats the noise of alternating runs, dropped otherwise.

Throughput in the window stretch is threads times the share on CPU divided by the CPU a transaction costs; a limit on one factor is not a ceiling.

### 6.2 A run on the maintainer's machine

Mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` to height 975073, `-dbcache=30000` (no lag run happens during the connect at that size: every coin, 166 million changes, is still unwritten at the stop height), 32 connect workers, `-par=16`, `-debug=coindb`, 32 vcores. Every run command carries `-disablewallet`: a loaded wallet past its birthday does per-transaction work on `BlockConnected` during a reindex and filled the validation-interface queue (hundreds of seconds of publisher wait in two runs). Bench per-block figures divide by windowed blocks. `-debug=bench` slows the publisher; sampled runs say so.

### 6.3 The harness

Scripts are in `/work/utxo-partial-order-harness/` (README). Chains D and DX are kept there too (`chains/`: the builder's datadir and `tip.txt`); the other chains regenerate from their scripts. Datadirs in use and binary snapshots live in a session's temporary directory and go with it; their numbers are in these notes. Marks and the standing caveat are in the header; in particular the harness chain's blocks are all allocation and its store is small, so its ratio for anything about a block's memory is an upper bound, not a prediction.

Settings that every harness run needs:

- The baseline is branch `utxo-partial-order-baseline` (326374edc0): master e211dd0186, the current cut's base, plus one probe commit that adds the series' `-test=bip30_off` to regtest, never for upstream. Built by `cpu/build_baseline.sh` with `build-bench`'s settings into `cpu/bin-baseline`. Every comparison against master uses it with the same options as the series; given no `-test` option it is plain master and serves the syncs. Straight master is no baseline on a regtest chain: it pays the BIP30 check on every block (7.7).

- A clean conf: a test-framework datadir carries `par=2` and `prevoutfetchthreads=1`.
- Regtest's consistency checks off: regtest alone defaults `-checkblockindex` and `-checkmempool` to every operation (`fDefaultConsistencyChecks`), so every measured node and every serving node gets `-checkblockindex=0 -checkmempool=0` (`run.sh`, `syncX.sh`).
- The BIP30 check off, through `-test=bip30_off` (regtest only, on the series since the fifth review, 4.11). Master has no such option, so master's runs pay the check, on one thread; the earlier `BENCH_NO_BIP30` probe switch is read by no current binary. Regtest, testnet4 and signet run the check on every block; mainnet only up to height 227,931 and from 1,983,702, the two repeat blocks aside (the BIP34 block is checked too: its parent has no ancestor at that height). `ConnectBlock` calls `HaveCoin` for every output a block creates, each a LevelDB `Get` that finds nothing under the database's one mutex, and from eight workers on the workers queue on it: `DBImpl::Get` is 7% of worker CPU at 4 workers and 42% at 16; a reindex runs 122 / 151 / 142 / 140 blocks/s at 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 workers with the check and 169 / 302 / 345 / 300 to 333 without (83 of 95 ms of worker time per block on chain C).
- `-dbcache=1024` in the network simulation; at 450 the window's byte cap made a false plateau (4.5).
- `-maxtipage=1` for a sync, or the client leaves initial block download once the tip is recent and no later block goes to the pool (`cpu/syncX.sh` passes it; its first runs on chain DX did not, left initial block download at chain D's end, connected the new heavy blocks outside the window at 22 blocks/s, and are void).
- A quiet host. The machine also runs other work (the maintainer's guix build on 18 November); a rate counts only with the foreign load during the run recorded and near its floor (`cpu/rounds.py`): about one core with nothing else on the machine, the driver's own polling. Idle sampled just before a run predicts nothing. The first run after a pause reads the block files cold and does not count (7.7).
- Pruning needs a sync: `-reindex-chainstate` refuses `-prune`. The minimum target is 550 MiB on every chain; `-fastprune` shrinks block files to 64 KiB and lowers regtest's prune-after height to 100 but leaves the target, and with 64 KiB files pruning is checked at nearly every block, a cadence no mainnet node has and one that would penalise master's prune flush far beyond mainnet. Chain D (428 MB of block and undo data) never prunes; chain DX does.
- The binding low-end corner is `-dbcache=30 -maxmempool=5`, because the idle mempool's allowance is lent to the coins cache; `-dbcache=50` alone never bound it. There the tip ran 18 blocks/s against 55 unbound, core at 38 ms CPU a block against 11: the low-end path's own cost, never looked at (5.2).
- Matched builds and settings: harness snapshots are clang builds (`build-bench`); GCC builds run about 7% slower on this harness and are not compared with them (the first cut's last harness check found its apparent regression to be exactly that). Compare runs only at matched `-dbcache`: on chain D's `-reindex-chainstate` 1024 against 2000 MiB moves the rate about 4%. A full reindex of chain D is bound by the block loader, not the connect (7.7).
- `taskset` does not change the detected core count; chain confs pin `par` and the workers (the `tune/` harness of 4.9).

Chains:

| Chain | What it is | What it cannot show |
|---|---|---|
| C | 500 blocks of 3000 two-input transactions, outputs spent 40 blocks later | nothing is deferred, the continuation never runs |
| D | `cpu/build_chain_d.py`: 300 heavy blocks of 3000 two-in two-out transactions, inputs 60% old, 30% one to twenty blocks old, 10% from the same block (assumed shares); defers about 12.6% of transactions at 16 workers | its coins come from fan-out transactions, so its shards are uneven |
| DX | `cpu/build_chain_dx.py`: chain D continued on a copy of its datadir, the coin pool rescanned from the UTXO set in 40 age groups; 1,500 more heavy blocks of D's shape (heights 2,782 to 4,281, built in 1,645 s), then 2,100 empty; 6,381 blocks, 2.51 GB of block and undo data in 16 file pairs, about 1.39 MB a heavy block | the same assumed shares as D; its top blocks are recent, so a sync needs `-maxtipage=1`; at `-prune=550` it prunes about a dozen times in its heavy section (inferred) |
| E | small blocks below the BIP34 height (4.6) | a block is so small that the serial threads compete with the workers (7.4) |
| M, N | the network simulation's chains (7.5) | |
| empty | `empty/build_chain_empty.py`: 40,000 coinbase-only blocks with timestamps in the past; `empty/run.py` follows the log and prints the per-thread split, `ab.sh` alternates master and the tip | only the fixed cost of a block; not a design input (C9) |

Drivers: `cpu/rounds.py` alternates binaries on chain D's `-reindex-chainstate` (through `runx.sh` and `run.sh`) from the rate line and prints the foreign load during each run; `cpu/syncX.sh` syncs any chain from one local peer running the baseline binary as plain master (`cpu/bin-base` is the first cut at 82a1b90f76 and `f14/bin-base` the machines port at 18dd95aeb5, neither is master), the client's options given per run, and reports prune events and the blocks directory's size (`cpu/syncD.sh` needs a series server binary that is gone). `cpu/ab.py` needs the sampler scripts, which were not kept, and `ab2.py`, `ab3.py`, `ab5.py`, `f14/*.sh` and `reqfix/syncn.sh` read log lines the consolidation removed (Worker phases, Connect pipeline, Block receive, Block cache:). Noise: only ratios of alternating runs in one session count, and below about 3% nothing is distinguished. The per-thread split comes from `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/schedstat`. "Window wait 0.00 s" under `-debug=bench` is the logging slowing the publisher. No perf on the development box (`perf_event_open` is refused under the seccomp filter).

| 16:00.92 | 17 Nov | `-dbcache=16000`, baseline for the asynchronous write trial | 19731.29 / 1237.52 s, 2182% | |
| 14:24.21 | 17 Nov | `-dbcache=16000`, the asynchronous write trial | 19730.96 / 1130.92 s, 2413% | 1.11x faster; the trial was dropped for its overrun of the memory budget |
| 26:47.77 | 17 Nov | `-dbcache=4000`, baseline | 20917.53 / 2156.67 s, 1435% | |
| 34:13.58 | 17 Nov | `-dbcache=4000`, the asynchronous write trial | 20734.91 / 1938.06 s, 1104% | 1.28x slower: the writer fell behind and the cache ran to 5146 MiB against 4276 |
| 13:13.01 | 17 Nov | tip 9f168d8c8a: transaction outputs in neighbouring buckets and the lighter create prefetch | 19636.90 / 1095.20 s, 2614% | "The previous run was not faster" |
| 13:02.47 | 17 Nov | tip 1a4521fc6b: all four store commits of the day | 19517.91 / 1125.38 s, 2638% | "the fastest run so far, but the difference was pretty small"; then 12:43.32 on the same tip, the reference of the 11:36.26 row |
The same 166,055,947 changes are flushed each time. Not on that machine: a friend's mainnet sync (7.5).

What the runs of 10 and 11 November established and later runs did not overturn:

- The publisher's own serial time was about 650 s over a run, 0.7 to 0.9 ms a block: a floor that more workers do not move.
- Master's shutdown write was 83% inside the database's write call; the 29:12 run's stall at exactly every 12th batch confirmed the mechanism of 4.8, and the stalls of 23:55 to 22:39 were a shard of about 43 MiB exceeding a batch (4.8). With four or more flush workers the flush is at the database's serial write.
- Moving the undo off the connector bought no wall time: the connector fell to 3 to 5% of a core and waits on the window about 90% of the late chain. The workers are the bound.
- The sweep's removal gave a third of the sweep's time; the rest came back in batch and cont, since the sweep was mostly work that has to be done somewhere. The maintainer the same day: "Nono, definitely keep the low-end hardware logic! We will move to that shortly."
- The 15,073 blocks above the assumevalid block (966143) ran at 124 blocks/s, 9.7% of the connect, never sampled.
- Freeing a published block was 14% of worker time, and the short sleeps (about 1470 a block) sat mostly in the free, cont and batch phases, inside deallocation of what another worker allocated; what followed is in 4.3. When the two CPU commits took 824 sleeps a block away the blocked time barely fell: idle time follows the number and kind of the sleeps, not their length (7.4). The bins pay about 3.5% of worker time; the own hash costs about 1.4% on a machine with cores idle for a hash pool, and paid where every core is busy.
- 256 shards with the record freed in place against 1024: the store-touching phases wait 4.3 ms a block more; an inference, worth at most 1.05x late.
- After the 22:39 run the maintainer: "I think this is looking pretty good."

The 16:36.45 run, the latest. Stretches in seconds, this run / 18:00.87 / the sampled depot run of 12 November (log span 1102.0 s): to 240,000 25.0 / 26.2 / 65.0; to 840,000 590.5 / 621.5 / 639.1; to 860,000 36.7 / 74.1 / 38.8; to the tip 299.1 / 321.4 / 320.0; shutdown 44.2 (flush of 166.1 million changes with 96.38 s summed in the database's write). 6.7 million entries at 240,000, no lag run, peak 28162 MiB (in the 18:00.87 run the kept markers were 24.2 million entries more from 240,000 on, about 3.4 GiB, the cache at its ceiling at 839,999 and a lag run of 33 s in the stretch to 860,000). At 950,000: Window wait 592.62 s; worker phases wall/CPU in ms a block read 5.61/4.73, fetch 1.55/1.25, core 7.86/7.06, batch 3.49/3.22, cont 4.58/3.58 (sum 23.09/19.84); Confirm 130.04 s, Publish finish 6.29, postprocess 17.55. Workers (32 on a machine 3.8% idle) on CPU 56% at 300,000, 89% at 550,000, 82% at 800,000, 92% at 950,000, plus 4.5 to 5% waiting for a core. The publisher is on CPU 10 to 20% and waits for a core 10 to 31%; its timers are wall and include that wait.

The 14:00.95 run (sampled, the series after pass 3). Stretches in seconds against 16:36.45: to 240,000 24.4 / 25.0; to 840,000 491.7 / 590.5; to 860,000 29.9 / 36.7; to the tip 257.5 / 299.1; shutdown 36.6 / 44.2 (flush 35.36 s, 102.17 s summed in the database's write).

Six-second thread samples, the 32 connect workers each: at 300,000 56.2% on CPU and 1.8% waiting for a core; at 550,000 87.1% and 4.7%; at 800,000 76.0% and 3.2%; at 950,000 89.0% and 6.8%. The machine at 550,000 and 950,000 is full: 28.2 and 28.7 cores busy, 33 threads runnable on 32, 4 to 8% idle. The publisher thread runs 16 to 26% and waits for a core 23 to 30% late. Worker CPU per block, cumulative at 950,000: 16.74 ms (read 3.27, fetch 1.18, core 5.99, batch 3.07, cont 3.23), wall 20.00 ms; between 800,000 and 950,000 the phases are off the CPU 16% of their wall, of which the samples put 5 to 7 points in waiting for a core, the rest spread over read, core, cont and fetch at 2 to 4 points each. 800,000 to 840,000 is the one stretch with slack (465 and 596 blocks/s against 670 before and after): idle 17%, I/O wait 5.1%, system 7.2%, the store growing 116.5 MiB a second; the cause is not separated. Minor faults there are 31,937 a second, about that growth in 4 KiB pages (29,800).

Over the whole run the picture is the opposite of what that one sample suggests: all threads together had 4.70 million minor faults at 950,000 (17.9 GiB if each were 4 KiB) for 27.8 GiB resident, where the three earlier sampled runs had 8.9 to 10.0 million (34 to 38 GiB) for 29 to 32 GiB. By stretch against the 16:36.45 run, in GiB of 4 KiB faults: to 300,000 1.1 / 3.3, to 550,000 +3.0 / +8.1, to 800,000 +5.1 / +10.5, to 950,000 +8.7 / +12.5. So roughly 45% of this run's memory arrived in larger units than 4 KiB, most of it early, less as free memory fell (10.8, 5.1, 1.1, 0.8 GiB free at the four samples): the huge page hint (5.4 S4) was very probably in the binary and granted in part, with fallback to small pages late. A first reading of the 800,000 sample alone as "no huge pages" was wrong. The 1.205x over 16:36.45 is therefore shared between pass 3 and the hint, not separated; whether the fallback or compaction costs time at 800,000 is not known (the machine's `thp_fault_alloc`, `thp_fault_fallback` and `compact_stall` counters would say).

Reading: from 400,000 on (92% of the wall before shutdown) the workers hold the machine; what is left on 32 hardware threads is CPU per block, which is work (deserialising 24% of worker CPU, core 36%, cont 18%, batch 16%, fetch 6%); no floor for it was ever measured on mainnet. The publisher's own time to 950,000 is 226 s wall, by sampled CPU nearer 80 to 160 s: the serial part a larger machine would meet first, 3.5x away by the wall figure. The flush is 4.4% of the run.

From the first report to this one 173,053 blocks and 394.5 million transactions in 161.6 s. The only run with stretch figures on record is the 14:00.95 one (an older tree), which stood at 840,000 after 516.1 s; this run was 6,083 blocks past that after 391.7 s, so at least 1.32x on that run to this height. The runs of 11:36.26 to 12:01.84 have no stretches read out, so nothing is said against them until the full time is in.

Run 32, third report the same day, with the maintainer's word on the binary ("Yes, this is my own application, tip is coins: a create that overwrote nothing is recorded as its outpoint alone"): 25497429f23f is the 36-commit series as of 60e21746db. At 08:08:01.24, 7:49 elapsed (468.5 s), height 913981, tx 1,239,702,679, progress 0.834644, cache 18256.8 MiB (170,415,240 txo). From the second report to this one 67,898 blocks and 223.6 million transactions in 76.8 s.

### 7.2 The flush: probes and the estimate

All probes throwaway, 12 million coins, on this VM; directions only.

- Order of the batches (32 MiB batches, on-disk database with the 8 MiB cache): hash order 28 s with 3.2 GB of compaction writes; disjoint descending batches unsorted inside 27.4 s with 0.86 GB; disjoint and sorted 5.7 s; the store's flush as built then 10.2 s. The time on the VM was the database's insertion of each key into its memtable, which a sorted batch makes about five times cheaper.
- Whole shards and four workers (a shard about 1.3 batches): 9.84 s before, 8.45 s without the cut inside a shard, 7.54 s with four workers as well. With four workers the flush is bound by the database's single writer.

The estimate for the maintainer's machine was about 40 s against 145 s; the 16:36.45 run measured 42.84 s.

### 7.3 CPU per transaction, the profile, the trials

The reference, chain C, 16 cores, all-thread CPU per transaction:

| run | blocks/s | threads on CPU | CPU per tx |
|---|---|---|---|
| pool off (`-prevoutfetchthreads=0`) | 25.1 | 1.49 | 19.8 µs (connector alone 12.9) |
| below the window (`-connectwindow=1`), 16 workers | 26.4 | 2.18 | 27.5 µs |
| window, 1 worker | 31.6 | 1.03 | 10.9 µs |
| window, 2 workers | 56.2 | 2.03 | 12.0 µs |
| window, 8 workers | 229.6 | 7.95 | 11.6 µs |
| window, 16 workers | 416.7 | 14.38 | 11.7 µs |

The window path's CPU per transaction is flat from one worker to sixteen and no higher than either serial path. On the maintainer's machine it was 13.5 to 22 µs then (per phase late: read 3.2, fetch 1.9, core 4.0, batch 4.0, cont 3.3, the block's free 2.4), of the size two threads per core and mainnet's larger set would give (inference).

Profile of the tip of 11 November (chain D, 16 workers, workers only). CPU: map lookups 33.3%, allocation and free 21.8%, SHA-256 5.9%, lock and yield 3.4%. Wall: 9.0% of samples in a system call, from the allocator's lock on cross-thread frees of nodes (`EraseDetached` under `SpendLocked` under `ApplyBatch`) and the shard lock taken by `PeekCoinFor` from core and cont. The duplicate `HaveInputs` in core is 0.54%.

The CPU probe on the maintainer's machine (14 November, beside the 14:18.66 run, commit 44). Counters over 5 s, all threads: instructions per cycle 0.49 at 600,000, 0.48 at 820,000, 0.41 at 900,000 and 2.26 at 970,000 (scripts); second-level data TLB misses 2.5, 2.2 and 3.0 million per CPU-second below assumevalid. The connect is bound by memory stalls, the script stretch by arithmetic (secp256k1 about 85% of worker samples there).

Huge pages: the process had 5.8 of 10.7 GiB anonymous memory in huge pages at 600,000, 10.5 of 22.7 at 820,000, 11.0 of 28.7 at 900,000 (54%, 46%, 38%); between 820,000 and 900,000 the store grew 6.0 GiB and 0.55 GiB of it was huge (269 huge faults granted, 10,026 fallen back). `enabled` and `defrag` are both `madvise`; free memory was 5.0, 7.0 and 1.1 GiB with 34, 20 and 20 GiB of page cache (the block files, read once), and the kernel reclaimed and compacted in the faulting threads (direct reclaim 23.6 million pages and 88,500 compaction stalls between 600,000 and 900,000). So the page cache of block files takes the memory the huge pages would need.

Worker samples at 900,000 by function (four workers, 3 s; 600,000 and 820,000 are alike): glibc malloc and free 26.1% (`_int_malloc` 6.4, `_int_free_merge_chunk` 5.8, `unlink_chunk` 4.9, `__libc_malloc2` 4.2, `cfree` 3.0, the rest 1.8), with the destructors that call them 6.7% more (`~vector<CTxIn>` 3.4, `CTransaction` 1.9, the bin's `Empty` 1.3); the callers the chains show are first the witness stacks (a vector per stack and one per item, allocated in `ReadBlock`, freed in the bin) and the per-block set of txids that `ConnectBlock` builds at the first deferred transaction (a node per transaction, at least 1.2%). The block view's map 17 to 18% (`try_emplace` 5.3 + 2.2 + 1.2, its pool's nodes 2.6, `ApplyChanges` walking it 4.7, part of the hasher's 2.8). The store 18% (`Detach` 4.6, `Map::find` 4.7, `ApplyBatch` 3.0, `FetchFor` 1.7, node allocation 1.1, try-lock 1.1, `try_emplace` 0.8); of `Map::find`, 0.85 points are single probes from `HaveInputs` called by `ConnectBlock` in the first pass, for coins the fetch had already not found; 1.04 are the continuation's by their chain, and the 2.12 whose chain ends at `HaveInputs < CheckTxInputs` are very likely the continuation's too, since in the first pass with deferral on `CheckTxInputs` finds every coin in the view (`ConnectBlock`'s own `HaveInputs` fetched it). So about 3.2 points are the continuation's single lookups, which the batched fetch for a continuation addresses (7.4), not the first pass. (An earlier reading here gave the first pass 3.0 points; it was wrong.) Size computation of transactions 2.9% (`SerializeTransaction<SizeComputer>`), SHA-256 2.3%, `GetSigOpCount` 1.5%. The call chains were cut at 1400 characters, so many allocations have no caller shown; the flat profile of all threads was lost below assumevalid to a sort by thread (fixed in the script).

Reading: the allocator's share at mainnet's scale is larger than the harness profile's 21.8% and it is not lock waits any more (7.4) but CPU in glibc's slow paths (chunks merged and unlinked, so not served by the per-thread cache). Three shares of about a fifth each (allocator, view map, store) and a huge page coverage of 38% late are what a run below 10 minutes has to come from; the script stretch (about 100 s) and the flush (36 s) have no lever.

The three trials of 14 November (chain D regenerated, 16 workers, `-dbcache=1024`, BIP30 switch, five alternating rounds, `cpu/ab2.py`; worker CPU in ms a heavy block from the log's phase lines):

| state | blocks/s, median | worker CPU | core | cont | deferred | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| tip 26bd9d762b | 510 | 26.9 to 27.2 | 11.6 to 11.7 | 2.1 to 2.3 | 107,000 to 113,000 | |
| + txid vector (commit 45) | 1.02x and 1.06x | 0.96x in both sessions | 11.0 to 11.1 | 2.11 | the same | on the series |
| + no second tier probe for an input the fetch found no entry for | 0.94x of the state above | 1.03x of the state above | 11.18 | 3.07 | 188,000 (1.76x) | dropped (86616d2b1a) |
| + input coins by position (commits 46, 47), without the skip | 1.086x of the tip, 1.07x of commit 45 | 0.91x of the tip, 0.95x of commit 45 | 9.83 (0.89x of commit 45) | 2.02 | the same | on the series |

Why the skip loses: the fetch runs at the start of a block's task and the single probe some milliseconds later, and in between lower blocks apply their creations. The second probe of the tier was finding about 80,000 of those coins a run; with the tier's answer remembered they are missed, the transaction is deferred and the continuation looks every one of its inputs up singly. The probe saved was 0.85 points at most. The skip was built so that only outpoints with no entry at all are remembered (a spent marker is read again) and only where deferral is on; it is correct and not worth having. The batched fetch for a continuation (commit 48, 1e17b4a574, 14 November; the maintainer: "Lets try out the batched continuation fetch"): `BlockApplyView::Prefetch(block, txs)` called by `RunContinuation()` before `FinishDeferredTransactions()`. Chain D against the 47-commit tip, same settings, five alternating rounds: cont CPU 2.00 to 1.82 ms a heavy block (0.91x), worker CPU 24.76 to 24.29 (0.981x), rate 1.012x (noise), deferred the same. Mainnet defers 27.2% against the chain's 12.6% and the continuation was 19% of worker CPU there, so the share addressed is larger, but after the span commits no harness ratio is carried over: the mainnet run decides.

The harness caveat of 6.3 holds for the two kept: a small store, so the ratios are upper bounds for mainnet. The maintainer's run with them (13:59.75, 7.1) showed user CPU 0.986x and no wall gain: the probes the span removes are repeats of a probe whose first hit took the memory stall, cheap where the first one is not, which the harness with its small store does not weigh (an inference from the 0.41 to 0.49 instructions per cycle, not measured).

Trials (on a throwaway branch):

| trial | chain D, 16 workers | 2 workers, cache not binding | 2 workers, cache binding | memory | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 pool per shard (upstream's `PoolResource`) | 1.21 to 1.26x | 1.19 to 1.21x | 1.07x | +10 to +40 MiB, pools 1.55 times the live nodes | dropped for its memory |
| T1p a thread keeps the nodes it frees, bound 1024 | 1.12x | 1.19x | 0.98x (noise) | as the tip | on the series (4.3) |
| T1p, bound 8192 | about 1.2x | 1.20x | 1.01x (noise) | +10 to +20 MiB | not taken |
| T3 plain shard mutex (retry lock removed) | 1.015x and 0.99x (noise) | 1.01x (noise) | | equal | held then: no measured loss on 16 cores, but a waiter sleeps more often (13 to 31 switches a block), which could show at more threads. Taken on 22 November after the maintainer's run at 32 workers, 10:33.33 (5.2, 7.1) |
| T4 duplicate `HaveInputs` | not built | | | | dropped, 0.54% |
| T2 batched fetch for a continuation | not built then; see 7.4 | | | | at the noise limit |

Block bins and own hash on chain C, reindex: 1.13x at sixteen workers, 1.08x at eight, with less CPU; neither alone does it (the bin alone 1.05x, own hash alone 0.90x at eight workers). Sync at four cores 1.04x. Pool on a CPU 79% to 88%, voluntary switches a block 85 to 21. With the two the best pool width is the core count and more is worse; before them more workers than cores gained a little because workers slept on hash futures.

#### The block file read-ahead (commit 46, 14 November)

The maintainer, on the dip between 600,000 and 800,000 (7.1): "Just try out the block reads and the serial publisher", memory growth being accepted as a cost. No code gave the operating system a read-ahead hint, and each block is read by its worker with an open, an 8 byte read and the block's read.
